Let's design local optimizer using additive gradient + Euclidean projection.

Pseudo:
def smooth_objective_and_grad(a, tau, M_shift=None):
    c = np.convolve(a,a)
    M = c.max()
    # For gradient, use weights over all k; can upshift to avoid overflow
    u = c - M
    e = np.exp(u/tau)
    q = e / e.sum()
    # If tau small, exp may underflow for nonactive; okay, sum maybe >0. If all underflow? e[active]=1 at max, sum>=1.
    g = 2.0 * np.correlate(q, a, 'valid')
    # Score = M (with S norm)
    return M, g

But for small tau and c differences moderate, okay. For tau very small <1e-3, gradient dominated by max constraints. Use active set weights to avoid underflow.

Projection onto simplex sum S:
def project_simplex(v, S):
    v = np.maximum(v,0)
    if v.sum()==0: return full(S/n)
    if v.sum()<=S: return v+(S-v.sum())/n?
But if any negative, set zero then add mass; okay. Better standard:
    x = v.copy()
    swe = 1 #? 
Standard algorithm for projection onto {x>=0, sum x=S}: sort x descending, find threshold. It handles positives/negatives. Implementation:
    u = np.sort(x)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    rho = np.nonzero(u * np.arange(1,len(u)+1) > (cssv - S))[0][-1]
    theta = (cssv[rho] - S) / (rho+1)
    return np.maximum(x - theta, 0)
This is O(n log n). For n=10000 and many steps, sort each step maybe costly. Binary search O(50n) might be okay; choose faster. For n=3000, sort cheap. For n=10000, sort 10k log ~1e5; 1000 steps 1e8 operations, okay maybe. Binary 50*10k=5e5 per step, 5e8 too high. We can optimize: use sort but cache? Since a changes slightly, threshold similar. Could implement Newton on threshold using quantile? Maybe sort is fine in C (Timsort) 10k maybe 0.1ms? 1000=0.1s? Actually np.sort 10k ~0.1ms? 1000=0.1s. Good. So use standard sort.

Implement functions:
- normalize(a, S): if a.size != n? ensure. a = np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<=0: full; return a/s*S.
- conv_max(a): return float(np.convolve(a,a).max()) maybe allocate c.
- smooth_grad(a, tau): returns c, J, g.
   c = np.convolve(a,a)
   M = c.max()
   # We want J = M + tau*log(sum(exp((c-M)/tau))). If tau very small, e may be [0..1]; sum>=1. Use np.exp((c-M)/tau, where=?) Vectorized.
   z = (c-M)/tau
   # to avoid overflow (z≤0), no overflow. Underflow okay.
   e = np.exp(z)
   s = e.sum()
   if s==0 or not finite: e = np.ones_like(c); e[imax]=? But z max 0 => at least one exp(0)=1, sum≥1.
   q = e/s
   g = 2.0 * np.correlate(q, a, 'valid')
   J = M + tau * np.log(s)
   return c, J, g, M
   Note: If tau small and many active with c close M, weights moderate.

- objective_score(a): use normalize? Since we keep normalized, score = max conv. But for evaluation, can call evaluate_sequence for final maybe not needed. We can compute raw: S2=sum^2; score=2*n*maxc/S2. If using S=√(2n), score=maxc. Ensure final return list of floats normalized doesn't matter; scoring invariant. Could return as is.

- project_simplex(v, S):
   v = np.asarray(v, dtype=float)
   if v.size ==0: return v
   # We want projection of v onto {x>=0, sum=S}
   # Standard:
   u = np.sort(v, kind='mergesort')[::-1]
   cssv = np.cumsum(u)
   ndim = len(u)
   cond = u * np.arange(1, ndim+1) > (cssv - S)
   # if no cond true? Happens if S > sum positives? Then all rho? Need handle.
   if not np.any(cond):
       # threshold negative, set all equal add
       return v + (S - v.sum())/ndim  # but ensure nonneg? If S positive and v could negative large? projection to positive sum: if all thresholds? Standard formula with rho=0? Let's robust:
   idx = np.max(np.nonzero(cond)[0])
   theta = (cssv[idx] - S)/(idx+1)
   return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
But if S smaller than sum of top? Standard works. Let's test simple v=[-5, -5], S=10. u=[-5,-5], cssv[-5,-10], cond: u*1 > cssv-S? -5 > -15 true, idx=1 theta=(-10-10)/2=-10, max(v-theta)=5 each. Good. If v all negative, works. If v=[100,0], S=1: u[100,0], cssv[100,100], cond i=1:100>99 true; i=2:0 >99 false; idx0 theta=(100-1)/1=99 => [1,0]. Good. If S=200, v sum100: u cond? i1:100> -100 true; i2:0>-100 true; idx1 theta=(100-200)/2=-50 => [150,50], projection of [100,0] to sum200 positive is [150,50]? Euclidean yes. Good.
   Need guard numerical: res.sum might slightly off; adjust max element.
   res = np.maximum(v-theta,0)
   diff = S - res.sum(); if abs>1e-9: res[res.argmax()] += diff; if res still negative due diff? okay.

- local_search(a0, tau_hi, tau_lo, eta0, t_budget, use_momentum=False, verbose):
   n=a0.size; S=np.sqrt(2*n)
   a = normalize(a0,S)
   best = a.copy(); best_M = maxconv(a)
   alpha=0.9? beta=0.7? 
   eta=eta0
   best_eta=eta
   lr_scale=1.0
   c,J,g,M=smooth_grad(a,tau_hi)
   v = np.zeros(n) if momentum
   last_M=best_M
   step=0
   no_improve=0
   while time<t_budget:
       elapsed fraction f = min(1, (time-t0)/t_budget)
       tau = tau_hi*(tau_lo/tau_hi)**f (geometric)
       # maybe recompute grad with current a,tau
       c,J,M,g = smooth_grad(a,tau)
       # compute direction
       if momentum:
           v = beta*v - g
           direction = v
       else:
           direction = -g
       # adaptive step length
       gnorm = np.linalg.norm(direction)
       if gnorm < 1e-12: break
       # choose lr = eta * norm_a / gnorm (for gradient descent direction -g; direction=-g norm=gnorm; step magnitude norm_a*eta)
       anorm = np.linalg.norm(a)
       lr = eta * anorm / gnorm
       # cap to avoid coordinate > some? If lr*max_g reasonable; lr = min(lr, 1.0/(max_abs_g +1e-12)*eta? But anorm/gnorm gives relative.
       # Backtracking line search on smooth J with Armijo using current g and Δ.
       accepted=False
       lr_trial=lr
       for _ in range(8):
           a_trial = project_simplex(a + lr_trial*direction, S)  # direction = -g or v
           # For Armijo with non-smooth projection, compute J_trial
           c_t,J_t,M_t,_ = smooth_grad(a_trial,tau) # recomputes gradient maybe expensive; we only need J, but function returns grad. Could make smooth_value.
           # But if direction not pure -g, use <g, Δ>
           delta = a_trial - a
           descent = -np.dot(g, delta)  # positive if moving against grad
           if J_t <= J + 0.25 * descent: # since J + α∇J·Δ, α=0.5? Actually sufficient J_t <= J + α g·Δ. g·Δ negative. Use 0.5.
                ...
           lr_trial *= 0.5
           if lr_trial < ... break
       if accepted:
           old_M=M
           a=a_trial
           M=M_t
           if M < best_M - 1e-12: best=a.copy(); best_M=M; no_improve=0; maybe increase eta
           else: no_improve +=1
       else:
           # fallback small step? or reduce eta
           eta *= 0.5
       # adapt eta
       if M_t < M: eta = min(eta*1.05, eta_max)
       else: eta = max(eta*0.7, eta_min)
       step+=1
   return best, best_M

Let's design mirror local search in detail:

def mirror_local_search(p0, tau_hi, tau_lo, eta0, t_budget, verbose=False):
    n=p0.size
    p = np.asarray(p0,float)
    p = np.maximum(p,0); s=p.sum(); if s<=0: p=ones(n)/n
    p = p/s
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    logp -= logp.max()  # for stability
    # We'll maintain p recomputed from logp each time.
    best = p.copy(); best_C = maxconv(p)
    eta = eta0
    beta=0.9? We can use momentum in logp: v=0; v = beta*v - g_centered; logp_trial = logp - eta*v? But line search.
    t0=time.monotonic
    step=0
    while time<t_budget:
       f=... tau = ...
       p = exp(logp) normalized (shift)
       c = np.convolve(p,p); M=c.max()
       e=np.exp((c-M)/tau); s=e.sum(); q=e/s; J=M+tau*log(s)
       g=2*correlate(q,p)
       # Center by weighted mean to avoid constant: gm = np.dot(p,g); g_c=g-gm
       pg = np.dot(p, g_c*g_c)
       if pg < 1e-30: break
       # base eta scaling: We want expected log change ~ maybe 1: eta*max|g_c| ~1. So eta_base = 1/(max|g_c|+eps). But line search can adapt.
       eta_max = 1.0 / (np.max(np.abs(g_c)) + 1e-12) * 0.5? Wait if g~1/n, eta_base~n/2. Good. If we have previous eta, update.
       # Try trial
       accepted=False
       eta_trial = eta
       for _ in range(10):
           logp_t = logp - eta_trial * g_c
           # maybe momentum? skip
           p_t = softmax(logp_t)
           c_t = np.convolve(p_t,p_t); M_t=c_t.max()
           # J_t
           e_t = np.exp((c_t-M_t)/tau); s_t=e_t.sum(); J_t=M_t+tau*np.log(s_t)
           # sufficient decrease
           if J_t <= J - 0.05 * eta_trial * pg: # conservative
               accepted=True; break
           eta_trial *= 0.5
       if not accepted:

def opt_euclid(a0, budget, tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.005, vrel=0.5, verbose=False):
    n=len(a0); S=np.sqrt(2*n)
    a=normalize_to_sum(a0,S)
    best=a.copy(); bestM=conv_max(a)
    c,J,g,M=smooth_grad(a,tau_hi)
    lr = 0.2 * np.linalg.norm(a)/max(np.linalg.norm(g),1e-12)
    last_a=a.copy(); last_g=g.copy(); step=0; t0=time.monotonic()
    while time<budget:
       f=... tau=...
       a = normalize? keep
       c,J,g,M=smooth_grad(a,tau)
       if np.linalg.norm(g)<1e-12: break
       # BB update for lr using current and last (before computing new grad? Use previous g)
       if step>0:
          s=a-last_a; y=g-last_g
          sy=np.dot(s,y)
          if sy>1e-12:
             lr_new=np.dot(s,s)/sy
             lr = min(max(lr_new, 0.1*lr), 5*lr)
       # Line search
       step_dir = -g
       accepted=False; lr_t=lr
       base_descent = np.dot(g, a) # no
       for _ in range(10):
           a_t = project_simplex(a + lr_t*step_dir, S)
           c_t, J_t, g_t, M_t = smooth_grad(a_t, tau) # for BB and accept; g_t maybe expensive but okay? But BB after accept saves.

           descent = -np.dot(g, delta)
           if J_t <= J + 0.5*descent:
               accepted=True
               last_a=a; last_g=g; # previous before update
               a=a_t; J=J_t; g=g_t; M=M_t
               break
           lr_t *=0.5

       # time check
    return best, bestM

```
def run_euclid(a0, budget_s, tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.01, lr_factor=1.0, verbose=False):
    if budget_s <= 0: return a0, conv_max_score
    n=a0.size; S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=normalize_sum(a0,S)
    best=a.copy(); bestM=conv_max(a)
    c=conv(a); M,J,q=weights; g=grad_from_q(q,a)
    anorm=np.linalg.norm(a); gnorm=np.linalg.norm(g)
    lr = 0.25 * anorm / max(gnorm,1e-12)
    # maybe cap lr to 1.0? previous absolute lr 1; if anorm/gnorm ~1, lr=0.25.
    last_a=a.copy(); last_g=g.copy()
    step=0; fail=0
    t0=time.monotonic()
    while time<t0+budget:
        frac=min(1, elapsed/budget)
        tau=tau_hi*(tau_lo/tau_hi)**frac
        # recompute q/g with current tau maybe; we have M,J,g from previous? If accepted last, we computed g? We'll recompute each loop for simplicity.
        c=M? 
        ...
        if finite false break
        if gnorm < 1e-14: break
        # BB step update
        if step > 0:
           s= a - last_a
           y= g - last_g
           sy=np.dot(s,y)
           if sy > 1e-14:
               lr_bb = np.dot(s,s)/sy
               if np.isfinite(lr_bb) and lr_bb >0:
                   lr = min(max(lr_bb, lr*0.25), lr*4.0)
           else:
               lr *= 0.7
        # line search
        accepted=False
        lr_t = lr
        for _ in range(12):
           a_t = project_simplex(a - lr_t*g, S)
           c_t = conv(a_t); M_t, J_t, _ = soft_value(c_t,tau)
           delta = a_t - a
           descent = -np.dot(g, delta)
           if (descent > 0 and J_t <= J + 0.5*descent) or (M_t < M - 1e-9):
               # accept
               last_a = a.copy(); last_g = g.copy()
               a = a_t
               M, J = M_t, J_t
               # update g? To avoid recompute next loop, compute q_t and g_t:
               q_t = soft_weights_from_c(c_t, tau)[1]
               g = grad_from_q(q_t,a)
               accepted=True
               break
           lr_t *= 0.5
           if lr_t < 1e-12*(anorm/max(gnorm,1)): break
        if accepted:
           if M < bestM - 1e-10:
               bestM=M; best=a.copy()
           # adapt lr: if accepted with full lr_t close to lr, increase slightly
           if lr_t >= 0.9*lr:
               lr *= 1.05
           else:
               lr = lr_t
           step +=1
           fail=0
        else:
           fail +=1
           lr *=0.5
           if fail > 20:
               # restart with small perturbation or break
               # maybe add random noise to escape
               a = project_simplex(a + 0.01*np.random.default_rng(step).standard_normal(n), S)
               c=conv(a); M=...
               fail=0; last_a=a; last_g=zeros
    # ensure best normalized
    best = normalize_sum(best,S)
    return best, float(2.0*n*max(conv(best,best))/np.sum(best)**2)
```

```
def soft_value(c, tau):
    M=float(c.max())
    z=(c-M)/tau
    # if tau <=0? handle
    e=np.exp(z)
    s=e.sum()
    J=M + tau*np.log(s)
    return M,J
```

```

For sparse gradient, need q. Could compute active count. `grad_from_q(q,a)`:
```
def grad_from_q(q,a):
    n=a.size
    tol=1e-10
    idx=np.nonzero(q>tol)[0]
    if idx.size ==0:
        # shouldn't; find max
    if idx.size < max(10, q.size//4):
        g=np.zeros(n)
        for k in idx:
            wk=q[k]
            if wk <=0: continue
            lo = k - (n-1)
            if lo < 0: lo=0
            hi = k
            if hi > n-1: hi=n-1
            start = k - hi
            end = k - lo + 1
            g[lo:hi+1] += wk * a[start:end][::-1]
        return 2.0*g
    else:
        return 2.0*np.correlate(q,a,'valid')
```
Check if idx size small but weights spread with small values below tol, okay. Use tol=1e-9 maybe. q sum1.

Python loop over 2400 for n=3000 might be slow (each slice length average 1500, 3.6M mult in C but 2400 Python loops). Might be okay but full correlate C 9M perhaps faster. Softmax log to sum S:
```
def logsoftmax_to_sum(loga, S):
    # loga 1d finite or -inf
    m = np.max(loga[np.isfinite(loga)]) if any else 0
    e = np.exp(loga - m)
    s = e.sum()
    if s == 0 or not finite:
       # uniform fallback
       return np.full(n, S/n)
    a = e / s * S
    return a
```
If loga has -inf, exp ->0.

Candidate evaluation:
```
def score_of(seq):
   if len==0: return inf
   # use our fast score (Sum norm)
   a=np.asarray(seq,float); a=np.maximum(a,0)
   s=a.sum(); if s<1e-9: return inf
   c=np.convolve(a,a); return float(2*a.size*c.max()/(s*s))
```

Now, mirror optimizer details. Could implement similar with time budget. Let's maybe not overinvest if Euclid sufficient. But mirror can help from Euclid best. We'll include a simpler mirror.

Mirror run:
```
def run_mirror(a0, budget_s, tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.01, beta=0.8, verbose=False):
   n,S,a=normalize
   loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)); loga-=loga.max()
   eta=0.5 / (max(abs grad?) unknown) set 20.0
   v=np.zeros(n)
   best...
   prev? no
   step=0; fail=0; t0=time
   while time:
      tau...
      c=conv(a); M,J,q,g=grad_from_c
      if gnorm<1e-14: break
      g_c = g - np.sum(a*g)/S
      v = beta*v - g_c
      maxv=np.max(np.abs(v))
      if maxv<1e-14: break
      eta_base = 0.5/maxv
      eta = min(max(eta, 0.2*eta_base), 10*eta_base)
      accepted=False
      eta_t=eta
      for _ in range(10):
         loga_t = loga - eta_t*v
         a_t = logrescale(loga_t,S)
         c_t=conv(a_t); M_t,J_t=soft_value(c_t,tau)
         if M_t < M - 1e-10 or J_t < J - 1e-10:
            accepted=True; break
         eta_t *=0.5
      if accepted:
         loga=loga_t; a=a_t; M/J update
         if M<best...
         # adapt eta
         if eta_t >= 0.9*eta: eta = min(eta*1.03, 20*eta_base)
         else: eta=eta_t
         fail=0
      else:
         fail +=1
         eta = max(eta_t, eta_base*0.05)
         if fail>20:
             # reset momentum and small perturbation
             v=zeros
             # perturb loga with small noise
             rng = np.random.default_rng(step)
             loga = loga + 0.05*rng.standard_normal(n)
             a=logrescale(loga,S)
             fail=0
      step+=1
   return best
```
Momentum in v: It is velocity in log gradient units. `v = beta*v - g_c`. If beta high, velocity scale can grow up to 1/(1-beta) times g. eta_base 0.5/maxv gives log step ~0.5. Good.

Potential problem: If v has large negative, loga - eta*v increases some coordinates; may blow range. line search rejects if not decrease. Could have loga range grow; shift each step: after accept, `loga -= loga.max()` to keep stable. Do that.

Need logrescale handle -inf. If loga max -inf (all zeros), fallback uniform.

```
def init_param(n, kind, seed=0):
    if kind==0: return ones
    t = np.linspace(0,1,n)
    if kind==1: return np.exp(-1.0*t)
    if kind==2: return np.exp(-2.0*t)
    if kind==3: return np.exp(-4.0*t)
    if kind==4: return 0.5*np.exp(-2*t)+0.5*np.exp(-2*(1-t))
    if kind==5: return 0.7*np.exp(-1.5*t)+0.3*np.exp(-1.5*(1-t))
    if kind==6: # chirp
       tt = t*2-1; return 1+0.5*np.cos(np.pi*tt*tt)
    if kind==7: # random smooth
       rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
       a = 1.0 + 0.5*rng.standard_normal(n)
       # low-pass circular filter: convolve with Gaussian? Use np.convolve(a, kernel, 'same')
       k = np.exp(-0.5*(np.arange(-20,21)/5)**2); k/=k.sum(); a=np.convolve(a,k,'same')
       return np.maximum(a,0.1)
```
These shapes scaled arbitrary; optimizer normalizes.

Let's think of using `scipy.signal.fftconvolve` for n>5000 to speed. We can import scipy? Allowed. But relying on scipy availability maybe okay. However direct considered. If we use FFT for conv, need real. Implement optional:
```
def convolve_fast(a):
    n=a.size
    if n < 5000: return np.convolve(a,a)
    # use numpy FFT to avoid scipy dependency
    N = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:2*n-1]
    return c
```
This uses O(n log n) and no scipy. For n=3000, N=8192, FFT maybe faster than direct? Could use always. But `irfft` returns length N, slice. Need ensure floating errors small negative? Convolution of nonnegative should be nonnegative, but numerical errors could be tiny negative. Use `np.maximum(c,0)`? That may affect gradient? small. Use direct for small to avoid FFT overhead and errors. For large, FFT. Let's implement `convolution(a)` top-level:
```
def convolution(a):
    n=a.size
    if n <= 4096:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    N = 1
    L = 2*n - 1
    while N < L: N <<=1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    c = c[:L]
    # remove tiny negatives
    # If min < -1e-9? maybe direct fallback? 
    if c.min() < -1e-7:
       # fallback direct? But direct could be slow; clip
       c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    else:
       c[c<0]=0.0
    return c
```
Need gradient correlate. For large n, we still use `np.correlate(q,a,'valid')` which is O(n^2) if full; would ruin FFT advantage. We need fast gradient if using FFT. Could compute grad via FFT too. Let's derive fast gradient term1_j = correlate(q,a,valid) length n, q length 2n-1, a length n. We can compute using FFT cross-correlation. Let N >= L+n-1 = 3n-2 to avoid circular alias for valid? For cross-correlation full length? Full cross-correlation of q and a length L-1? Actually q length L=2n-1, a length n. We need r[k]=∑_{m=0}^{n-1} q[m+k] a[m] for k=0..L-n = n-1. Let's define thresholds:
```
def convolution(a):
   n=a.size
   if n <= 4096: return np.convolve(a,a)
   N=1; L=2*n-1; while N<L: N*=2
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]
   # clip tiny negative
   c = np.where(c<0,0,c) if c.min()<0? np.maximum(c,0)
   return c
```
Clipping all c to nonnegative may slightly bias but fine. If numerical errors bigger, still.

`grad_from_q`:
```
def grad_from_q(q,a):
   n=a.size
   # sparse if active small
   idx = np.nonzero(q > 1e-9)[0]
   if idx.size ==0: idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(q))])
   if idx.size <= 64 and idx.size * n < 2_000_000:
       ...
   elif n <= 4096:
       return 2.0 * np.correlate(q, a, 'valid')
   else:
       N=1; Lfull=3*n-2; while N<Lfull: N*=2
       fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       fh=np.fft.rfft(q[::-1],N)
       conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fh,N)
       term = conv[2*n-2 : n-3 : -1]
       # remove tiny imaginary? irfft real
       # numerical noise maybe term has small errors; no clip? gradient can be any sign. Use as is.
       return 2.0 * term
```

Implement perturbation top-level:
```
def perturb_sequence(seq, S=None, sigma=0.02, seed=0):
   a=np.asarray(seq,float); a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); n=a.size
   if s<=0: return a
   if S is None: S=np.sqrt(2*n)
   a = a/s*S
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   noise = rng.standard_normal(n) * (sigma * S / np.sqrt(n))
   a = project_simplex(a + noise, S)
   return a
```
This adds L2 noise. Use sparingly.

Maybe after each final round if score unchanged, perturb and run short. But don't lose best; update keeps best.

def run_euclid(a0, budget_s, tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.01, rel_lr=0.3, verbose=False):
    n = len(a0)
    S = np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a = normalize_sum(np.maximum(a0,0.0).astype(float), S)
    best = a.copy()
    best_M = float(convolution(a).max())
    best_score = 2.0*best_M  # because normalized to S
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + budget_s
    c = convolution(a)
    M, J, q, g = grad_from_c(c, a, tau_hi)
    anorm = np.linalg.norm(a)
    gnorm = np.linalg.norm(g)
    lr = rel_lr * anorm / max(gnorm, 1e-12)
    prev_a = None; prev_g = None; prev_tau = None
    step=0; fail=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline: break
        frac = min(1.0, (now-t0)/max(budget_s,1e-6))
        tau = tau_hi * (tau_lo/tau_hi)**frac
        if tau < 1e-9: tau=1e-9
        c, M, J, g = grad_from_c(c? no, need c fresh? We have c from previous a; a may have changed at accept and we might not have c for new tau? We can compute c fresh each loop for simplicity.)
```
To avoid extra, at top: `c = convolution(a)`, then `M,J,q,g = weights_grad(c,a,tau)`. This is one conv and weights.
```

        gnorm = np.linalg.norm(g)

def soft_weights_grad(c, a, tau):
    M = float(c.max())

    s = e.sum()

Now, possible issue with loga and momentum: If we perturb loga, need recompute a. Use `a = logrescale(loga,S)`.

Top-level `logrescale`:
```
def logrescale(loga, S):
    n=loga.size
    finite = np.isfinite(loga)
    if not finite.any():
       return np.full(n, S/n)
    m = np.max(loga[finite])
    e = np.exp(loga - m)
    s = e.sum()
    if s ==0 or not np.isfinite(s): return np.full(n,S/n)
    a = e * (S/s)
    return a
```
If loga has -inf, exp(-inf)=0.

Now, candidate initialization `init_param`: Need ensure positive and non-empty. For FFT random smooth, use `np.fft.irfft`? Simpler Gaussian kernel:
```
def init_random_smooth(n, seed):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = 1.0 + 0.4*rng.standard_normal(n)
    k = np.exp(-((np.arange(-20,21))/8.0)**2)
    k /= k.sum()
    a = np.convolve(a, k, mode='same')
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.05)
    return a
```

```
def normalize_sum(arr, S):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(a.size, S / max(a.size,1), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (S / s)
```
If arr length zero? return empty? But callers ensure nonempty. In `normalize_sum`, if n=0, S/0 divide; guard. Use `n=a.size; if n==0: return a`.

```
def project_simplex(v, S):
    x = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n=x.size
    if n==0: return x
    # sort
    u = np.sort(x)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.nonzero(u * np.arange(1,n+1) > (cssv - S))[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
        # all? fallback
        res = x + (S - x.sum())/n
        return np.maximum(res,0.0) * (S / max(np.maximum(res,0.0).sum(),1e-300))
    r = idx[-1]
    theta = (cssv[r] - S) / (r+1)
    res = np.maximum(x - theta, 0.0)
    s = res.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(n, S/n)
    return res * (S/s)
```
This sort each call; if v contains NaN? Project inputs should finite. If NaN, sort bad. Ensure finite: `x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=-1e6)`? Could hide issues. Add at start:
```
if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=-1e6)
```

`convolution`: For n small direct, for large FFT. Need ensure input contiguous? `a=np.ascontiguousarray(a)`. Return float64 length L.
```
def convolution(a):
    a = np.ascontiguousarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([], dtype=float)
    if n <= 4096:
        c = np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
        L=2*n-1; N=1
        while N < L: N <<=1
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:L]
    # Clip small negative
    minc = c.min()
    if minc < 0:
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c
```
For large FFT, numerical errors could make max slightly higher due positive noise? negligible.

`grad_from_q`:
```
def grad_from_q(q, a):
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([], dtype=float)
    tol = 1e-9
    idx = np.nonzero(q > tol)[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
        # use max only
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(q))], dtype=int)
    if idx.size <= 64 and idx.size * n <= 2000000:
        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for k in idx:
            wk = float(q[k])
            if wk <= 0: continue
            lo = k - (n - 1)
            if lo < 0: lo = 0
            hi = k
            if hi > n - 1: hi = n - 1
            if lo > hi: continue
            start = k - hi
            end = k - lo + 1
            # a[start:end][::-1]
            g[lo:hi+1] += wk * a[start:end][::-1]
        g *= 2.0
        if np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            return g
    if n <= 4096:
        g = 2.0 * np.correlate(q, a, 'valid')
        if np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            return g
    # FFT fallback for large
    Lfull = 3*n - 2
    N = 1
    while N < Lfull: N <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(np.ascontiguousarray(a), N)
    fh = np.fft.rfft(np.ascontiguousarray(q[::-1]), N)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fh, N)
    term = conv[2*n-2 : n-3 : -1]
    g = 2.0 * term
    if np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        return g
    # ultimate fallback if n small
    if n <= 10000:
        g = 2.0 * np.correlate(q, a, 'valid')
    else:
        g = np.zeros(n)
    return g
```
Need be careful: if `idx.size <=64` but `q` has many tiny weights below tol whose sum maybe significant if tau large? If tau large, q uniform, idx size=2n-1 (since >1e-9? for n=3000, 5999/6000 weights ~1.7e-4 >tol, not sparse). For tau small, tail below tol negligible. Good.

Potential bug in FFT slice for n=1? Lfull=1, N=1, slice `2*n-2=0`, `n-3=-2`; conv[0:-2:-1] weird. We can handle n==1 separately: conv length1, grad=2*q0*a0. But n likely >1. For robustness, in grad_from_q if n==1: return np.array([2*q[0]*a[0]]). Also project, etc. Add.

For n=2, slice stop n-3=-1? Wait n=2 => n-3=-1. Python slice `conv[2 : -1 : -1]` would go indices 2,1? Stop -1 means index N-1? Actually negative stop confusing. For n=2, we need indices 2,1. Slice `conv[2*n-2 : n-3 : -1]` = `conv[2 : -1 : -1]`. In Python, stop -1 means index -1 (last) inclusive? For negative step, sequence stops when index <= stop? Let's test mentally: list len5, slice [2:-1:-1] gives indices 2,3? I'm not sure. Python slice with start=2, stop=-1 (converted to len-1=4), step=-1: start 2, step -1, condition i > stop, but start<stop -> empty. So bad. We derived stop = n-3 = -1 should be index -1? We need stop just before last index? For n=2, indices 2,1, stop should be 0 (exclude). Formula n-3 = -1; but as positive stop 0? In Python, if we use `conv[2*n-2 : max(n-3, -1) : -1]` with n=2, max(-1,-1) = -1 bad. We need stop = n-3 if n-3 >=0 else -1? Let's derive: We want length n from start=2n-2 down to n-2 inclusive. For n=2, end=0? Wait formula end = n-2 =0. I earlier said indices 2,1? Let's recalc n=2: L=3, Lfull=3. term1 j=0: k=L-1-j=2; j=1: k=1? But n-2=0? Contradiction. Let's recompute L=2n-1=3, L-1=2. Let's derive: term1_j = conv_full(L-1 - j). L-1=2n-2. j=0 =>2n-2; j=n-1=>2n-2-(n-1)=n-1. So slice indices `2n-2` down to `n-1` inclusive. Length n. Stop should be `n-2` (exclusive). For n=3: indices4,3,2? n-1=2, yes. For n=2: indices2,1, stop=0. So slice `conv[2*n-2 : n-2 : -1]`. For n=2 stop=0, works. For n=1 stop=-1 bad; handle. Good. Earlier final formula should be `n-2`, not n-3. Let's verify n=3: conv[4:1:-1] indices4,3,2 good. Use `stop = n - 2` (for n>=2, stop >=0 for n>=2? n=2 stop=0; n=3 stop=1). Good.

Now, `grad_from_c`:
```
def grad_from_c(c, a, tau):
    M = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    if tau <= 0: tau=1e-9
    z = (c - M) / tau
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        e = np.zeros_like(c); e[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0; s=1.0
    q = e/s
    J = M + tau * float(np.log(s))
    g = grad_from_q(q,a)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        g = np.zeros_like(a)
    return M,J,q,g
```
`soft_value` similar without q.

Potential overflow in log(s) if s huge? s ≤ length c due e≤1, length up to 24000, log fine.

Now, score_of:
```
def score_of(seq):
    a=np.asarray(seq,dtype=float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.minimum(a,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-9: return np.inf
    c=convolution(a)
    M=float(c.max())
    return float(2.0*a.size*M/(s*s))
```
Matches eval clipping. If sequence length huge and FFT, okay. If a contains >1000, clip. Our values small.

`update_best`:
```
def update_best(best_seq, best_score, seq):
    if seq is None: return best_seq, best_score
    try:
       sc = score_of(seq)
    except Exception:
       return best_seq,best_score
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
       # store normalized? return copy float list? Keep numpy.
       return np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64).copy(), float(sc)
    return best_seq,best_score
```
No closure.

`resample_sequence`:
```
def resample_sequence(arr, n):
    a=np.asarray(arr,float).ravel()
    a=np.maximum(a,0); 
    if a.size==0: return ones(n)
    m=a.size
    if m==n: return a
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,m)
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    b=np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return np.maximum(b,0)
```

`perturb_sequence`:
```
def perturb_sequence(seq, sigma=0.02, seed=0):
   a=np.asarray(seq,float).ravel(); n=a.size
   S=np.sqrt(2*n)
   a=normalize_sum(a,S)
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   noise=rng.standard_normal(n) * (sigma * S / np.sqrt(n))
   return project_simplex(a+noise,S)
```

`logrescale_to_sum` as above.

Let's write `run_euclid` in full mentally:
```
def run_euclid(a0, budget_s, tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.01, rel_lr=0.3, verbose=False):
    a = a0
    if a is None or len(a)==0:
        return None, np.inf
    n = a.size
    S = np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a = normalize_sum(a, S)
    best = a.copy()
    best_M = float(convolution(a).max())
    best_score = best_M  # normalized score
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, budget_s)
    c = convolution(a)
    M, J, q, g = grad_from_c(c, a, tau_hi)
    anorm = float(np.linalg.norm(a))
    gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
    lr = rel_lr * anorm / max(gnorm, 1e-12)
    prev_a = None; prev_g = None; prev_tau = None
    step = 0; fail = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline: break
        frac = min(1.0, (now - t0) / max(budget_s, 1e-12))
        tau = tau_hi * (tau_lo / tau_hi) ** frac
        if tau < 1e-9: tau = 1e-9
        c = convolution(a)
        M, J, q, g = grad_from_c(c, a, tau)
        if not (np.isfinite(M) and np.isfinite(J)):
            break
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
        if gnorm < 1e-14:
            break
        anorm = float(np.linalg.norm(a))
        # BB
        if prev_a is not None and prev_tau is not None:
            if abs(tau - prev_tau) <= 0.25 * max(tau, 1e-12):
                svec = a - prev_a
                yvec = g - prev_g
                sy = float(np.dot(svec, yvec))
                if sy > 1e-14 and np.isfinite(sy):
                    ss = float(np.dot(svec, svec))
                    bb = ss / sy
                    if bb > 0 and np.isfinite(bb):
                        lr = min(max(bb, 0.25 * lr), 4.0 * lr)
        lr_before = lr
        base_min = 1e-6 * anorm / max(gnorm, 1e-12)
        accepted = False
        lr_t = lr
        for _ in range(10):
            a_t = project_simplex(a - lr_t * g, S)
            if a_t is None: break
            c_t = convolution(a_t)
            M_t, J_t = soft_value(c_t, tau)
            # accept if smooth or exact improves
            if M_t < M - 1e-10 or J_t < J - 1e-10:
                # also maybe check not catastrophic? 
                _, _, _, g_t = grad_from_c(c_t, a_t, tau)
                prev_a = a.copy()
                prev_g = g.copy()
                prev_tau = tau
                a = a_t
                M = M_t; J = J_t; g = g_t
                accepted = True
                break
            lr_t *= 0.5
            if lr_t < base_min:
                break
        if accepted:
            if M < best_M - 1e-10:
                best_M = M
                best = a.copy()
                best_score = best_M
            lr = lr_t
            if lr_t >= 0.9 * lr_before:
                lr = min(lr * 1.03, anorm / max(gnorm, 1e-12) * 5.0)
            fail = 0
            if verbose and step % 50 == 0:
                elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
                print(f"  euclid step {step} t={elapsed:.0f}s M={M:.6f} best={best_M:.6f} lr={lr:.4g} tau={tau:.3g}")
        else:
            fail += 1
            lr = max(lr_t, base_min)
            if fail >= 20:
                a = perturb_sequence(a, sigma=0.02, seed=step)
                c = convolution(a)
                M, J, q, g = grad_from_c(c, a, tau)
                prev_a = None; prev_g = None; prev_tau = None
                fail = 0
        step += 1
    best = normalize_sum(best, S)
    final_score = score_of(best) # uses raw
    if not np.isfinite(final_score): final_score = best_M
    return best, float(final_score)
```
Potential: In accept, we recompute `g_t` from c_t with same tau, but `J_t,M_t` from soft_value. If `grad_from_c` computes slightly different J due exp again, no matter. But we could avoid recompute by using weights from c_t? We can make `grad_from_c` and use its M,J. Let's simplify: In line search, call `grad_from_c(c_t,a_t,tau)` only when accept, but before accept we need M_t,J_t; soft_value does exp. Could instead call `grad_from_c` for every trial? That computes g and correlate, expensive. So keep soft_value. On accept duplicate exp okay.

But if accept condition uses J_t from soft_value and then grad_from_c maybe returns M' slightly different due numerical. We set M=M_t (soft) not M'. Fine.

We need define `soft_value` before.

Potential issue: If line search accepts a trial with M_t lower but J_t not due tau? okay. If large step causes projection to a far point with slightly lower M but bad for future, BB reset? It might still be okay. Could require `M_t <= M` (decrease). We have.

What if `lr_t` becomes very small and no accept; fail perturbation after 20. Good.

Now, `run_mirror` code:
```
def run_mirror(a0, budget_s, tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.01, beta0=0.85, beta1=0.6, verbose=False):
   if a0 None: return
   n; S
   a=normalize_sum(a0,S)
   # ensure positive floor
   floor = 1e-12 * S / max(n,1)
   a = np.maximum(a, floor)
   a = normalize_sum(a, S)
   loga = np.log(a) - np.log(a).max()
   v = np.zeros(n)
   eta = 1.0
   best=a.copy(); best_M=convolution(a).max(); best_score=best_M
   t0; deadline
   step=0; fail=0
   while time<deadline:
      frac=...
      tau=...
      beta = beta0 - (beta0-beta1)*frac
      c=convolution(a); M,J,q,g=grad_from_c(c,a,tau)
      if not finite break
      gnorm=...
      if gnorm<1e-14 break
      # center g with weighted mean under a/S
      mean_g = float(np.dot(a, g)) / S
      g_c = g - mean_g
      v = beta * v - g_c
      maxv = float(np.max(np.abs(v)))
      if maxv < 1e-14: break
      eta_base = 0.5 / maxv
      if step == 0:
         eta = eta_base
      else:
         # bound between 0.2 and 8 of base
         eta = min(max(eta, 0.2 * eta_base), 8.0 * eta_base)
      accepted=False
      eta_t=eta
      # line search in log space
      for _ in range(10):
          loga_t = loga - eta_t * v
          a_t = logrescale_to_sum(loga_t, S)
          c_t = convolution(a_t)
          M_t,J_t = soft_value(c_t,tau)
          if M_t < M - 1e-10 or J_t < J - 1e-10:
             loga = loga_t
             a = a_t
             M=M_t; J=J_t
             # shift loga
             mn = np.max(loga[np.isfinite(loga)])
             loga = loga - mn
             accepted=True
             break
          eta_t *= 0.5
      if accepted:
         if M < best_M - 1e-10:
             best_M=M; best=a.copy(); best_score=best_M
         if eta_t >= 0.9*eta:
             eta = min(eta*1.03, 20*eta_base)
         else:
             eta = eta_t
         fail=0
         if verbose...
      else:
         fail+=1
         eta = max(eta_t, 0.1*eta_base)
         if fail >= 20:
             v=np.zeros(n)
             # small perturbation in log space
             rng = np.random.default_rng(step)
             loga = loga + 0.05 * rng.standard_normal(n)
             a = logrescale_to_sum(loga, S)
             c=convolution(a); M,J,q,g=grad_from_c(c,a,tau)
             fail=0
      step+=1
   best=normalize_sum(best,S); final_score=score_of(best); return
```
Need in log perturbation avoid -inf? loga finite due floor. Good.

`logrescale_to_sum` if loga all -inf? not.

Potential issue: In mirror, using `v = beta*v - g_c` but `g_c` has units gradient; after accept with small eta, v remains previous gradient direction; next gradient maybe similar. Good.

Momentum may overshoot; line search on log changes. If fails many, reset.

Let's draft:
```
def propose_candidate():
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + 2350.0
    parent = None
    try:
       p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       if p.size > 0:
          p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
          p = np.maximum(p,0.0)
          p = np.minimum(p,1000.0)
          if p.sum() > 1e-9:
             parent = p
    except Exception:
       parent = None

    if parent is not None:
       best_seq, best_score = update_best(None, np.inf, parent)
    else:
       best_seq = init_param(3000, 6, 0)
       best_seq, best_score = update_best(None, np.inf, best_seq)
    print(f"initial score {best_score:.6f} n={len(best_seq)}")

    # phase 1 polish best
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 120:
        bud = min(360.0, max(60.0, rem * 0.18))
        # euclid
        seq1, sc1 = run_euclid(best_seq, bud * 0.6, tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.02, rel_lr=0.3, verbose=True)
        best_seq, best_score = update_best(best_seq, best_score, seq1)
        print(f"after euclid {best_score:.6f}")
        rem=...
        if rem > 60:
            seq2, sc2 = run_mirror(best_seq, min(bud*0.4, rem*0.4), tau_hi=0.6, tau_lo=0.01, beta0=0.85, beta1=0.6, verbose=True)
            update
    # phase2 explore
    rem=...
    if rem > 240:
       n_base = len(best_seq)
       n_large = int(min(12000, max(n_base*2, 6000)))
       # maybe if n_base large, n_large same? 
       candidates = []
       if parent is not None:
          candidates.append(resample_sequence(parent, len(parent)))
          if n_large != len(parent): candidates.append(resample_sequence(parent, n_large))
       for kind in [4, 5, 6, 7]:
          candidates.append(init_param(n_base, kind, seed=kind*7+1))
       if n_large != n_base:
          candidates.append(init_param(n_large, 4, seed=101))
          candidates.append(init_param(n_large, 7, seed=102))
       # Limit total explore time
       total_exp = min(rem * 0.22, 420.0)
       per = max(18.0, total_exp / max(1, min(len(candidates), 6)))
       cand_count=0
       for cseq in candidates:
          rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
          if rem < per + 60 or cand_count >= 6: break
          b = min(per, rem*0.35, 90.0)
          if b < 10: break
          cand_count += 1
          print(f"explore cand {cand_count} n={len(cseq)} t={b:.0f}s")
          # choose budget split
          seqe, sce = run_euclid(cseq, b*0.65, tau_hi=0.8, tau_lo=0.02, rel_lr=0.25, verbose=False)
          best_seq,best_score=update_best(...)
          if run_euclid returned None skip
          rem=...
          if rem > b*0.35:
             seqm, scm = run_mirror(seqe, b*0.35, tau_hi=0.5, tau_lo=0.01, beta0=0.8, beta1=0.55, verbose=False)
             update
    # phase3 final polish
    rem=...
    stages = [
       (0.6, 0.02, 'euclid', 0.3),
       (0.4, 0.008, 'mirror', 0.8),
       (0.3, 0.005, 'euclid', 0.25),
       (0.2, 0.003, 'mirror', 0.7),
       (0.12, 0.0015, 'euclid', 0.2),
       (0.08, 0.001, 'mirror', 0.6),
       (0.05, 0.0007, 'euclid', 0.15),
    ]
    for tau_hi, tau_lo, method, lr in stages:
       rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
       if rem < 120: break
       bud = min(600.0, rem * 0.45)
       if bud < 30: continue
       if method == 'euclid':
          seq, sc = run_euclid(best_seq, bud, tau_hi=tau_hi, tau_lo=tau_lo, rel_lr=lr, verbose=True)
       else:
          seq, sc = run_mirror(best_seq, bud, tau_hi=tau_hi, tau_lo=tau_lo, beta0=0.85, beta1=0.6, verbose=True)
       old = best_score
       best_seq,best_score = update_best(best_seq,best_score,seq)
       print stage...
       # optional perturb if no improvement and rem > 300
       rem=...
       if best_score >= old - 1e-8 and rem > 300:
          seqp, scp = run_euclid(perturb_sequence(best_seq, sigma=0.03, seed=int(time.monotonic()*1000)%10000), min(120, rem*0.15), tau_hi=max(0.1,tau_hi), tau_lo=tau_lo, rel_lr=0.3, verbose=False)
          best_seq,best_score = update_best(...)
    # Final evaluate
    final_list = [float(max(0.0, x)) for x in best_seq]
    try:
       es = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
       if not np.isfinite(es): ...
    except Exception:
       es = best_score
    # If evaluate score worse than best_score? Due clipping? return best_seq maybe. But if best_seq has negative? no.
    return final_list
```

`init_param`: Need `seed` can be used for random kinds. Use `np.random.default_rng(seed)`. For n=0? return empty. Kinds ensure positive. For edge singular kind maybe large values; normalize later.

Let's implement kinds:
```
def init_param(n, kind, seed=0):
    if n <=0: return np.array([])
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t = (np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    if kind ==0: return np.ones(n)
    if kind ==1: return np.exp(-0.5*t)
    if kind ==2: return np.exp(-1.0*t)
    if kind ==3: return np.exp(-2.0*t)
    if kind ==4: return 0.7*np.exp(-1.0*t)+0.3*np.exp(-1.0*(1.0-t))
    if kind ==5: return 0.5*np.exp(-2.0*t)+0.5*np.exp(-2.0*(1.0-t))
    if kind ==6:
       base = 1.0 + 0.5*np.cos(np.pi*(2.0*t-1.0)**2)
       base += 0.05*rng.standard_normal(n)
       return np.maximum(base,0.1)
    if kind ==7:
       return _random_smooth(n,0.3,seed)
    if kind ==8:
       return _random_smooth(n,0.7,seed+1)
    if kind ==9:
       return 1.0/(0.2 + np.abs(2.0*t-1.0)) # edge-ish? Actually peaks at edges, dips center.
    return np.ones(n)
```
Kind9 peaks at |2t-1|=0? t=0.5 center! Wait denominator 0.2+|2t-1|: min center 0.2 => peak center, edges 1.2; not. For edge peaks use `1/(0.2 + min(t,1-t))`. That singular edges. Could be bad but explore.
```
return 1.0/(0.2 + np.minimum(t,1.0-t))
```
Values ~5 at edges, 2.5 center. okay.

Define `_random_smooth` top-level (though underscore okay) or inside? Top-level.
```
def _random_smooth(n, amp, seed):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = 1.0 + amp * rng.standard_normal(n)
    k = np.exp(-0.5 * (np.arange(-20,21)/6.0)**2)
    k /= k.sum()
    a = np.convolve(a, k, mode='same')
    return np.maximum(a, 0.05)
```
Allowed helper top-level. No closures.

  line search: a_t, c_t, M_t,J_t
       accept: loga=loga_t; a=a_t; c=c_t; M/J...
  if fail perturb: c=convolution(a)
```
Good.

This reduces FFT transforms: top no conv, accept line search trial conv only. Each accepted step conv for trial + next loop no conv. But line search rejected trials conv. Average 1-2 conv per step plus no extra. Great.

In line search, `soft_value(c_t,tau)` computes max and exp; no gradient. Good.

Now, in `run_euclid` BB, `prev_g` old gradient at prev_a computed with prev_tau. If tau same-ish, okay.

Potential issue: `g` from top with current c/tau; line search Armijo not used, so gradient only for direction. Good.

Now, update `run_euclid` code accordingly.

Let's write final functions with this optimization.

In `run_euclid`, after fail and perturbation, set `c = convolution(a)` and continue; `step +=1`. At top next, recompute grad.

In `run_euclid`, after accepted, we don't recompute best_score until end, but best_M and M score. Good.

Now, maybe `score_of(best)` at end uses direct/Fourier and may be slightly lower/higher. okay.

Now, in `run_euclid`, if `M` not finite at top break. But c finite.

Now, let's consider effect of using `c` from previous a with tau changed but a same. Good.

Now, implement `grad_from_c` to accept c and a. It recomputes q/g every loop; could be costly due exp/correlate. But no conv. Good.

In full dense grad FFT, each top loop 3 transforms, line search trials 1 conv each (2 transforms). Good.

Now, let's think about if `q` weights for top should be based on c from current a. Yes.

Now, code `soft_value` and `grad_from_c` share exp; if top and line search use same tau, duplicate exp for c_t? okay.

Potential issue: In line search, `M_t,J_t=soft_value(c_t,tau)`. For accept, if M_t lower but J_t maybe not due numerical; okay. If M_t same but J_t lower, accept.

Now, run_euclid initial best: `best_M = float(c.max())`. c computed. Good.

Now, maybe after many steps, `c` and `a` may be large length; memory okay.

Let's ensure `normalize_sum` at end of run doesn't alter score? It normalizes to S. If best already S, okay. If due projection scaling maybe sum exact S. Good.

Now, one more mathematical check: The objective is scale invariant. We normalize to S=√(2n), so score=max_c. But when we resample best to larger n, we normalize in optimizer. Score for different n. Good.

Now, let's consider if `score_of` for normalized S sequence should equal max_c, but due FFT maybe. okay.

Now, let's write final code. Ensure no type annotations that could cause issues? We can use simple.

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing
    2 * n * max(conv(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

Strategy:
  * Work on sequences normalized to sum sqrt(2n), so the objective equals
    max(conv(a,a)).
  * Use several local search methods:
      - projected Euclidean gradient/line search with Barzilai-Borwein retuning
      - mirror/max-sum updates in log-space with momentum
  * Use exact smooth log-sum-exp gradients, with a sparse/FFT acceleration for
    the convolution-gradient term.
  * Start from the provided state-of-the-art sequence, try upsampled copies and
    a few smooth parametric random initializations, then leave most of the time
    for polishing the best point.
"""

import time
import numpy as np


def normalize_sum(a, S):
    """Normalize a non-negative vector to sum S."""
    if a is None:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return x
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return x * (float(S) / s)


def project_simplex(v, S):
    """Project onto {x >= 0, sum(x)=S}."""
    x = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return x
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=-1e6)

    u = np.sort(x)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    ar = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * ar > (cssv - float(S))
    idx = np.nonzero(cond)[0]

    if idx.size == 0:
        res = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
        s = float(res.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            res = np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            rem = float(S) - s
            if rem > 0.0:
                res = res + rem / float(n)
            elif rem < 0.0:
                res = res * (float(S) / s)
            res = np.maximum(res, 0.0)
            s2 = float(res.sum())
            if s2 > 0.0:
                res = res * (float(S) / s2)
        return res

    r = int(idx[-1])
    theta = (cssv[r] - float(S)) / float(r + 1)
    res = np.maximum(x - theta, 0.0)
    s = float(res.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return res * (float(S) / s)


def convolution(a):
    """
    Linear convolution a*a.
    Uses direct method for modest n and FFT for larger n.
    """
    x = np.ascontiguousarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    if n <= 4096:
        c = np.convolve(x, x)
    else:
        L = 2 * n - 1
        N = 1
        while N < L:
            N <<= 1
        fa = np.fft.rfft(x, N)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]

    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size and c.min() < 0.0:
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def grad_from_q(q, a):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k q_k conv(a,a)_k, for full linear convolution.

    For full convolution, d/d a_j of that sum is
        2 * sum_{r=0}^{n-1} q_{j+r} a_r,
    i.e. 2 * correlate(q, a, 'valid').
    """
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([2.0 * float(q[0]) * float(a[0])], dtype=np.float64)

    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.ascontiguousarray(a, dtype=np.float64)

    tol = 1e-9
    idx = np.nonzero(q > tol)[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(q))], dtype=int)

    # Very few active coefficients: sparse accumulation is cheap.
    if idx.size <= 64 and idx.size * n <= 2_000_000:
        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for k in idx:
            wk = float(q[k])
            if wk <= 0.0:
                continue
            lo = k - (n - 1)
            if lo < 0:
                lo = 0
            hi = k
            if hi > n - 1:
                hi = n - 1
            if lo > hi:
                continue
            start = k - hi
            end = k - lo + 1
            g[lo:hi + 1] += wk * a[start:end][::-1]
        g *= 2.0
        if np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            return g

    if n <= 4096:
        g = 2.0 * np.correlate(q, a, 'valid')
        if np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            return g

    # FFT cross-correlation for large n.
    Lfull = 3 * n - 2
    N = 1
    while N < Lfull:
        N <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fh = np.fft.rfft(q[::-1], N)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fh, N)
    term = conv[2 * n - 2:n - 2:-1]

    if term.size != n:
        if n <= 10000:
            g = 2.0 * np.correlate(q, a, 'valid')
        else:
            g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        g = 2.0 * np.asarray(term, dtype=np.float64)

    if np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        return g

    if n <= 10000:
        try:
            return 2.0 * np.correlate(q, a, 'valid')
        except Exception:
            pass
    return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)


def soft_value(c, tau):
    """
    Returns (M, J) where M=max(c) and J = tau logsumexp(c/tau),
    shifted by M for stability.
    """
    if c.size == 0:
        return 0.0, 0.0
    tau = max(1e-12, float(tau))
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M):
        return M, M
    z = (c - M) / tau
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        emax = int(np.argmax(c))
        e = np.zeros_like(c, dtype=np.float64)
        e[emax] = 1.0
        s = 1.0
    J = M + tau * float(np.log(s))
    return M, J


def grad_from_c(c, a, tau):
    """Compute softmax weights, smooth value J, and gradient from c=conv(a,a)."""
    if c.size == 0:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
        return 0.0, 0.0, np.array([], dtype=np.float64), np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    tau = max(1e-12, float(tau))
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M):
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
        return M, M, np.zeros(a.size, dtype=np.float64), np.zeros(a.size, dtype=np.float64)

    z = (c - M) / tau
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        e = np.zeros_like(c, dtype=np.float64)
        e[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        s = 1.0

    q = e / s
    J = M + tau * float(np.log(s))
    g = grad_from_q(q, a)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        g = np.zeros_like(a, dtype=np.float64)
    return M, J, q, g


def score_of(seq):
    """
    Fast local scorer matching the task evaluator except for very large values.
    """
    if seq is None:
        return np.inf
    a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return np.inf
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = np.minimum(a, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-9:
        return np.inf
    c = convolution(a)
    M = float(c.max())
    return float(2.0 * a.size * M / (s * s))


def update_best(best_seq, best_score, seq):
    """Return updated best sequence/score."""
    if seq is None:
        return best_seq, best_score
    try:
        sc = score_of(seq)
    except Exception:
        return best_seq, best_score
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
        return arr, float(sc)
    return best_seq, best_score


def resample_sequence(arr, n):
    """Resample a sequence to length n with linear interpolation."""
    if arr is None or n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == n:
        return a.copy()
    if a.size == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64)

    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    b = np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    return np.maximum(b, 0.0)


def _random_smooth(n, amp, seed):
    """Smooth random positive initialization."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = 1.0 + float(amp) * rng.standard_normal(n)
    k = np.exp(-0.5 * (np.arange(-20, 21) / 6.0) ** 2)
    k = k / k.sum()
    a = np.convolve(a, k, mode='same')
    return np.maximum(a, 0.05)


def init_param(n, kind, seed=0):
    """Parametric positive initializations."""
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)

    if kind == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if kind == 1:
        return np.exp(-0.5 * t)
    if kind == 2:
        return np.exp(-1.0 * t)
    if kind == 3:
        return np.exp(-2.0 * t)
    if kind == 4:
        return 0.7 * np.exp(-1.0 * t) + 0.3 * np.exp(-1.0 * (1.0 - t))
    if kind == 5:
        return 0.5 * np.exp(-2.0 * t) + 0.5 * np.exp(-2.0 * (1.0 - t))
    if kind == 6:
        base = 1.0 + 0.5 * np.cos(np.pi * (2.0 * t - 1.0) ** 2)
        base = base + 0.05 * rng.standard_normal(n)
        return np.maximum(base, 0.1)
    if kind == 7:
        return _random_smooth(n, 0.35, seed)
    if kind == 8:
        return _random_smooth(n, 0.75, seed + 101)
    if kind == 9:
        # modest edge emphasis
        return 1.0 / (0.25 + np.minimum(t, 1.0 - t))
    return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)


def perturb_sequence(seq, sigma=0.02, seed=0):
    """Small random feasible perturbation of a sequence."""
    if seq is None:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    a = normalize_sum(a, S)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    noise = rng.standard_normal(n) * (float(sigma) * S / float(np.sqrt(n)))
    return project_simplex(a + noise, S)


def logrescale_to_sum(loga, S):
    """Convert log-vector to a positive vector summing to S."""
    n = loga.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.any(np.isfinite(loga)):
        return np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    m = float(np.max(loga[np.isfinite(loga)]))
    e = np.exp(loga - m)
    e = e * np.isfinite(loga)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return e * (float(S) / s)


def run_euclid(a0, budget_s, tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.01, rel_lr=0.3, verbose=False):
    """
    Projected Euclidean gradient method with line search and BB retuning.
    Sequence is normalized to sum sqrt(2n), so max(conv) is the score.
    """
    if a0 is None or budget_s <= 0:
        if a0 is None:
            return None, np.inf
        a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0:
            return None, np.inf
        n = a.size
        S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
        a = normalize_sum(a, S)
        return a, score_of(a)

    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return None, np.inf
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    a = normalize_sum(a, S)

    c = convolution(a)
    M0 = float(c.max())
    best = a.copy()
    best_M = M0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    rel_lr = max(1e-4, float(rel_lr))

    M, J, q, g = grad_from_c(c, a, tau_hi)
    anorm = float(np.linalg.norm(a))
    gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
    lr = rel_lr * anorm / max(gnorm, 1e-12)
    if not np.isfinite(lr) or lr <= 0.0:
        lr = 1e-3

    prev_a = None
    prev_g = None
    prev_tau = None
    step = 0
    fail = 0
    max_back = 8 if n <= 5000 else 6

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline:
            break
        frac = min(1.0, (now - t0) / max(float(budget_s), 1e-12))
        tau = float(tau_hi) * (float(tau_lo) / float(tau_hi)) ** frac
        if tau < 1e-9:
            tau = 1e-9

        M, J, q, g = grad_from_c(c, a, tau)
        if not (np.isfinite(M) and np.isfinite(J)):
            break
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
        if gnorm < 1e-14:
            break
        anorm = float(np.linalg.norm(a))

        # Barzilai-Borwein retuning, only when tau is nearly the same.
        if prev_a is not None and prev_g is not None and prev_tau is not None:
            if abs(tau - prev_tau) <= 0.25 * max(tau, 1e-12):
                svec = a - prev_a
                yvec = g - prev_g
                sy = float(np.dot(svec, yvec))
                if sy > 1e-14 and np.isfinite(sy):
                    ss = float(np.dot(svec, svec))
                    bb = ss / sy
                    if bb > 0.0 and np.isfinite(bb):
                        lr = min(max(bb, 0.25 * lr), 4.0 * lr)

        max_lr = 5.0 * anorm / max(gnorm, 1e-12)
        min_lr = 1e-6 * anorm / max(gnorm, 1e-12)
        lr = min(max(lr, min_lr), max_lr)
        lr_before = lr

        accepted = False
        lr_t = lr
        for _ in range(max_back):
            a_t = project_simplex(a - lr_t * g, S)
            if a_t.size != n:
                break
            c_t = convolution(a_t)
            M_t, J_t = soft_value(c_t, tau)

            ok = False
            if M_t < M - 1e-10:
                ok = True
            elif M_t <= M + 0.02 and J_t < J - 1e-10:
                ok = True

            if ok:
                prev_a = a.copy()
                prev_g = g.copy()
                prev_tau = tau
                a = a_t
                c = c_t
                M = M_t
                J = J_t
                accepted = True
                break

            lr_t *= 0.5
            if lr_t < min_lr:
                break

        if accepted:
            if M < best_M - 1e-10:
                best_M = M
                best = a.copy()
            if lr_t >= 0.9 * lr_before:
                lr = min(lr * 1.03, max_lr)
            else:
                lr = lr_t
            fail = 0
            if verbose and (step == 0 or step % 100 == 0):
                print(
                    f"  euclid n={n} step={step} t={time.monotonic()-t0:.0f}s "
                    f"M={M:.6f} best={best_M:.6f} lr={lr:.4g} tau={tau:.3g}"
                )
        else:
            fail += 1
            lr = max(lr_t, min_lr)
            if fail >= 20:
                a = perturb_sequence(a, sigma=0.02, seed=step + 1)
                c = convolution(a)
                M, J, q, g = grad_from_c(c, a, tau)
                prev_a = None
                prev_g = None
                prev_tau = None
                fail = 0

        step += 1

    best = normalize_sum(best, S)
    final_score = score_of(best)
    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        final_score = best_M
    if verbose:
        print(f"  euclid finished n={n} steps={step} score={final_score:.6f} time={time.monotonic()-t0:.0f}s")
    return best, float(final_score)


def run_mirror(a0, budget_s, tau_hi=1.0, tau_lo=0.01, beta0=0.85, beta1=0.60, verbose=False):
    """
    Mirror/log-space descent with momentum.  Sequence is normalized to
    sum sqrt(2n); max(conv) is the score.
    """
    if a0 is None or budget_s <= 0:
        if a0 is None:
            return None, np.inf
        a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0:
            return None, np.inf
        n = a.size
        S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
        a = normalize_sum(a, S)
        return a, score_of(a)

    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return None, np.inf
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    a = normalize_sum(a, S)

    floor = 1e-12 * S / float(n)
    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    a = normalize_sum(a, S)
    loga = np.log(a)
    loga = loga - float(np.max(loga))
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    eta = 1.0

    c = convolution(a)
    M0 = float(c.max())
    best = a.copy()
    best_M = M0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    step = 0
    fail = 0
    max_back = 8 if n <= 5000 else 6

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline:
            break
        frac = min(1.0, (now - t0) / max(float(budget_s), 1e-12))
        tau = float(tau_hi) * (float(tau_lo) / float(tau_hi)) ** frac
        if tau < 1e-9:
            tau = 1e-9

        beta = float(beta0) - (float(beta0) - float(beta1)) * frac

        M, J, q, g = grad_from_c(c, a, tau)
        if not (np.isfinite(M) and np.isfinite(J)):
            break
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
        if gnorm < 1e-14:
            break

        mean_g = float(np.dot(a, g)) / S
        g_c = g - mean_g
        v = beta * v - g_c
        maxv = float(np.max(np.abs(v)))
        if maxv < 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(maxv):
            break

        eta_base = 0.5 / maxv
        if step == 0:
            eta = eta_base
        else:
            eta = min(max(eta, 0.2 * eta_base), 8.0 * eta_base)
        eta = min(eta, 3.0 / maxv)

        accepted = False
        eta_t = eta
        for _ in range(max_back):
            loga_t = loga - eta_t * v
            a_t = logrescale_to_sum(loga_t, S)
            c_t = convolution(a_t)
            M_t, J_t = soft_value(c_t, tau)

            ok = False
            if M_t < M - 1e-10:
                ok = True
            elif M_t <= M + 0.02 and J_t < J - 1e-10:
                ok = True

            if ok:
                loga = loga_t
                a = a_t
                c = c_t
                M = M_t
                J = J_t
                finite = np.isfinite(loga)
                if np.any(finite):
                    loga = loga - float(np.max(loga[finite]))
                accepted = True
                break

            eta_t *= 0.5

        if accepted:
            if M < best_M - 1e-10:
                best_M = M
                best = a.copy()
            if eta_t >= 0.9 * eta:
                eta = min(eta * 1.03, 3.0 / maxv)
            else:
                eta = eta_t
            fail = 0
            if verbose and (step == 0 or step % 100 == 0):
                print(
                    f"  mirror n={n} step={step} t={time.monotonic()-t0:.0f}s "
                    f"M={M:.6f} best={best_M:.6f} eta={eta:.4g} tau={tau:.3g}"
                )
        else:
            fail += 1
            eta = max(eta_t, 0.1 * eta_base)
            if fail >= 20:
                v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                rng = np.random.default_rng(step + 1)
                loga = loga + 0.05 * rng.standard_normal(n)
                a = logrescale_to_sum(loga, S)
                c = convolution(a)
                M, J, q, g = grad_from_c(c, a, tau)
                fail = 0

        step += 1

    best = normalize_sum(best, S)
    final_score = score_of(best)
    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        final_score = best_M
    if verbose:
        print(f"  mirror finished n={n} steps={step} score={final_score:.6f} time={time.monotonic()-t0:.0f}s")
    return best, float(final_score)


def propose_candidate():
    """Main search entry point."""
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + 2350.0

    parent = None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if p.size > 0:
            p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
            p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
            p = np.minimum(p, 1000.0)
            if float(p.sum()) > 1e-9:
                parent = p
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None:
        best_seq, best_score = update_best(None, np.inf, parent)
    else:
        best_seq = init_param(3000, 6, 0)
        best_seq, best_score = update_best(None, np.inf, best_seq)

    if best_seq is None or len(best_seq) == 0:
        best_seq = init_param(3000, 0, 0)
        best_seq, best_score = update_best(None, np.inf, best_seq)

    print(f"initial best score={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_seq)}")

    # Phase 1: strong polishing of the best parent / initial sequence.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 150:
        bud = min(480.0, max(120.0, rem * 0.22))
        seq1, sc1 = run_euclid(
            best_seq,
            bud * 0.6,
            tau_hi=1.0,
            tau_lo=0.02,
            rel_lr=0.30,
            verbose=True,
        )
        best_seq, best_score = update_best(best_seq, best_score, seq1)
        print(f"after phase1 euclid: score={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_seq)}")

        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem > 80:
            seq2, sc2 = run_mirror(
                best_seq,
                min(bud * 0.4, rem * 0.35),
                tau_hi=0.6,
                tau_lo=0.01,
                beta0=0.85,
                beta1=0.60,
                verbose=True,
            )
            best_seq, best_score = update_best(best_seq, best_score, seq2)
            print(f"after phase1 mirror: score={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_seq)}")

    # Phase 2: moderate exploration from a few diverse initializations and
    # upsampled versions of the current best.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 260:
        n_base = int(len(best_seq))
        n_large = int(min(10000, max(2 * n_base, 5000)))
        if n_large <= n_base:
            n_large = min(10000, n_base + 1000)

        candidates = []
        candidates.append(resample_sequence(best_seq, n_large))

        if parent is not None:
            candidates.append(resample_sequence(parent, n_large))
            if int(parent.size) != n_base:
                candidates.append(resample_sequence(parent, int(parent.size)))

        for kind in (4, 5, 6, 7, 9):
            candidates.append(init_param(n_base, kind, seed=kind * 17 + 3))

        if n_large != n_base:
            candidates.append(init_param(n_large, 4, seed=103))
            candidates.append(init_param(n_large, 7, seed=104))

        valid = [c for c in candidates if c is not None and len(c) > 0]

        total_exp = min(rem * 0.20, 380.0)
        max_cand = max(2, int(total_exp // 70) + 1)
        max_cand = min(max_cand, len(valid))
        per = max(18.0, total_exp / max(1, max_cand))

        count = 0
        for cseq in valid[:max_cand]:
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 140 or count >= max_cand:
                break
            b = min(per, rem * 0.35, 90.0)
            if b < 12:
                break
            count += 1
            print(f"explore {count}/{max_cand} n={len(cseq)} budget={b:.0f}s")

            seqe, sce = run_euclid(
                cseq,
                b * 0.65,
                tau_hi=0.8,
                tau_lo=0.02,
                rel_lr=0.25,
                verbose=False,
            )
            best_seq, best_score = update_best(best_seq, best_score, seqe)

            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem > b * 0.35 and seqe is not None:
                seqm, scm = run_mirror(
                    seqe,
                    b * 0.35,
                    tau_hi=0.5,
                    tau_lo=0.01,
                    beta0=0.80,
                    beta1=0.55,
                    verbose=False,
                )
                best_seq, best_score = update_best(best_seq, best_score, seqm)

        print(f"after exploration: score={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_seq)}")

    # Phase 3: long focused polishing with decreasing smoothing scales.
    stages = [
        (0.50, 0.020, 'euclid', 0.30),
        (0.35, 0.008, 'mirror', 0.85),
        (0.25, 0.005, 'euclid', 0.25),
        (0.15, 0.003, 'mirror', 0.80),
        (0.10, 0.0015, 'euclid', 0.20),
        (0.07, 0.0010, 'mirror', 0.75),
        (0.05, 0.0007, 'euclid', 0.15),
    ]

    for tau_hi, tau_lo, meth, lr in stages:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 150:
            break
        bud = min(600.0, rem * 0.45)
        if bud < 40:
            continue

        old = best_score
        if meth == 'euclid':
            seq, sc = run_euclid(
                best_seq,
                bud,
                tau_hi=tau_hi,
                tau_lo=tau_lo,
                rel_lr=lr,
                verbose=True,
            )
        else:
            seq, sc = run_mirror(
                best_seq,
                bud,
                tau_hi=tau_hi,
                tau_lo=tau_lo,
                beta0=0.85,
                beta1=0.60,
                verbose=True,
            )
        best_seq, best_score = update_best(best_seq, best_score, seq)
        print(f"final {meth} tau=[{tau_hi},{tau_lo}] score={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_seq)}")

        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem > 320 and best_score >= old - 1e-7:
            seed = int((time.monotonic() + t_start) * 1000) % 1000000
            seqp, scp = run_euclid(
                perturb_sequence(best_seq, sigma=0.03, seed=seed),
                min(160.0, rem * 0.15),
                tau_hi=max(0.10, tau_hi),
                tau_lo=tau_lo,
                rel_lr=0.30,
                verbose=False,
            )
            best_seq, best_score = update_best(best_seq, best_score, seqp)
            if best_score < old:
                print(f"perturb improvement: {best_score:.6f}")

    # Finalize output.
    if best_seq is None or len(best_seq) == 0:
        best_seq = init_param(3000, 0, 0)
    final = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    final = np.maximum(final, 0.0)
    final = np.nan_to_num(final, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    final = np.minimum(final, 1000.0)

    s = float(final.sum())
    if s < 1e-6:
        final = np.full(max(final.size, 1), 1.0, dtype=np.float64)

    out = [float(x) for x in final]
    try:
        es = evaluate_sequence(out)
        if np.isfinite(es):
            print(f"final evaluate_sequence score={es:.6f} n={len(out)}")
        else:
            print("final evaluate_sequence non-finite")
    except Exception:
        print(f"final internal score={best_score:.6f} n={len(out)}")

    return out
```
